# HyperDrive V2 current release gates

AWS actual V2 partial graph acknowledgement fence verified (2026-10-16): two sync/async cases enable both failover and replication, acknowledge real root PutItem, then delegate real child PutItem/HTTP200 and inject original IOException at its SDK acknowledgement boundary. Exactly2commits; V2 returns error/not-saved with partial acknowledged-root identity and original uncertain child table/key/cause. Caller root identity published, child/leaf identities not published. Independent consistent reads prove root+child exist with correct native parent link; provider-parent read proves no leaf; SQLite root/child absent and ZERO secondary manager requests. Known synthetic root/child cleanup only after those assertions. Current native35/35 passes11s, zero skips, retaining prior33. No production change needed. Controlled SDK boundary, NOT physical wire-loss/durable recovery/all graph failure classes/full interface/live AWS/hosted certification.

AWS↔SQLite actual V2 generated graph replication verified (2026-10-16): four primary-provider×sync/async routed SaveHolon cases enable replication and request recursive children with initially empty root/child/leaf identities. Result has no error/warning and IsSaved; both actual stores independently reconstruct same3node tree through provider-child loading, exact generated child/leaf IDs, names and parent links. Current AWS native33/33 passes12s, zero skips; previous31 retained. Same fixed SQLite routing fixture reused, refuses prior evidence, removed only after complete verification/deactivation. No production change required for these cases. This narrows normal V2 recursive graph replication only; mixed-depth/options, native mid-graph unknown outcome, concurrent modification, durability/history/full interfaces/live service/hosted release still OPEN.

AWS/DynamoDB graph-save options implemented (2026-10-16): direct SaveHolon previously ignored saveChildren/recursive/depth; batch also published planned source IDs before acknowledgement. Eight real native sync/async cases cover root-only, direct children, depth1 and unlimited recursion. Six selected-child cases failed before fix (25pass/6fail). One planned batch path now validates selected references/IDs/parents/depth/serialization before writes, allocates detached snapshots, writes parent-before-child through existing direct SDK save with children disabled, publishes each source ID/parent/key only after acknowledgement, skips descendants of failed parents and stops on uncertain mutations. Current AWS native31/31pass10s, zero skips; independent consistent item reads prove every selected node and native parent field, selected source identity publication, unselected identities unchanged; provider-child readback reconstructs exact selected tree. Unit25/25pass307ms includes zero-write/zero-ID-publication cycle/duplicate/null/conflicting-parent preflight and acknowledged-root/uncertain-child stop proof.

An intermediate readback test incorrectly expected embedded navigation. Shared OasisJson explicitly excludes holon navigation and stores relationships by IDs; test corrected to assert empty embedded-only loading and exact provider-child loading, not introduce a parallel serializer. Graph writes are sequential, NOT atomic. Native mid-graph commit-loss/concurrency, all continueOnError combinations, V2 graph routing/replication, history/full interfaces/live AWS/hosted release remain OPEN. Existing point V2/fault/lifecycle tests retained.

DynamoDB receipt-vs-request evidence corrected (2026-10-16): reconciliation exception previously copied AmazonServiceException.RequestId into INativeMutationReconciliationRequired.OriginalTransactionId, so shared collector could advertise an HTTP request correlation as a native transaction receipt. New official SDK exception regression failed before fix (20pass/1fail). OriginalTransactionId now stays empty; additive SdkRequestId retains original request correlation and InnerException unchanged. Shared collector returns no fabricated transaction IDs while reconciliation-required remains true. Unit21/21 passes293ms; updated actual AWS native23/23 passes11s, zero skips. Does not supply a durable transaction receipt/recovery mechanism or close hosted/full-provider gates.

AWS/DynamoDB write publication ordering fixed (2026-10-16): profile/detail/direct holon saves previously published generated caller IDs (and profile/holon provider keys) BEFORE native acknowledgement. Six sync/async×record-kind cases delegate PutItem to healthy official SDK, assert real HTTP200, then throw one controlled original IOException at acknowledgement boundary. All6 reproduced mutated caller IDs before fix (17pass/6fail). Shared DynamoDB paths now serialize detached canonical snapshots and publish source IDs/keys plus IsSaved only after acknowledgement. Current AWS native23/23 passes10s, zero skips; each new case proves exactly1actual commit, same original cause/table/candidate key, reconciliation marker, independent consistent GetItem existence and unchanged caller identities/keys. Known synthetic committed item cleanup only after assertions. Controlled SDK boundary, NOT physical wire-loss/durable recovery/all interfaces/live AWS IAM/service/hosted release certification. Existing V2 races/replication/fences retained.

Google direct holon publication ordering fixed (2026-10-16): direct SaveHolon(saveChildren:false) also assigned caller identity/provider key before SDK acknowledgement. Two new actual reserved-loopback outage cases reproduced nonempty generated caller IDs after failed sync/async save (15pass/2fail). The existing direct path now materializes a detached canonical snapshot and publishes only identity/provider key back to the original source after acknowledged native write. Six write-outage/healthy-publication cases now cover profiles/details/holons; current complete native17/17 passes26s, zero skips, retaining graph/routing/fault/lifecycle cases. Exception preserves actual attempted document path and candidate identity; no hidden retries or alternate store. Durable unknown-outcome recovery/full interfaces/live Google/hosted certification remain open.

Google avatar write publication ordering fixed (2026-10-16): profile/detail saves previously assigned generated caller IDs (and profile provider key) before native acknowledgement. Saves now materialize detached existing canonical Avatar/AvatarDetail snapshots, write those through official SDK, and publish caller identity/key only after acknowledged SetAsync. Four real SDK reserved-loopback18083 failures (profile/detail×sync/async) verify error/reconciliation marker, fully qualified attempted document path with nonempty candidate GUID, unchanged caller IDs/profile key and retained inventory/XP. Separate explicit healthy operations then prove generated IDs published and matching independently read native documents; no automatic provider retry added. Native15/15 passes22s, zero skips. Initial two runs failed a TEST assumption that DocumentPath was collection-relative; corrected to actual fully qualified /documents/ path before caller-state assertions could run. Do not count those as reproduced production failures. Uncertain outcomes still require durable reconciliation; no physical post-commit ack-loss/all-interface/live service/hosted certification claim.

Google native holon projection invariant fixed (2026-10-16): SaveHolon wrote native isDeleted=!IsActive while canonical core IsDeleted derives from DeletedDate. Four active/deleted combinations independently compare actual Firestore fields to canonical data_json. Before fix inactive/undeleted and active/deleted cases failed (9pass/2fail); native mapping now uses holon.IsDeleted. Current11/11 native passes14s, zero skips, all previous routed lifecycle/fault/conflict cases retained. This fixes native write projection, not existing-record migration or a new read-visibility policy; no production dataset touched. Full interface/history/concurrency/recovery/live Google and hosted gates remain OPEN.

Google inactive-vs-deleted invariant fixed (2026-10-16): core HolonBase.IsDeleted is DeletedDate != DateTime.MinValue, not !IsActive. Firestore deletion incorrectly interpreted an inactive undeleted profile as a committed tombstone (empty timestamp) and an inactive undeleted detail as a conflict. New actual native sync/async cases reproduced both failures with "Tombstone requires a nonempty UTC commit timestamp"; transaction path now uses existing IsDeleted for tombstone selection/conflicts. Current native7/7 passes14s, zero skips; raw pre-delete reads prove inactive/no deletion, post-delete pair preserves actor/fields and valid shared UTC timestamp, repeat is idempotent and hard deletion removes both. Previous V2/conflict/selector tests retained. Not all save/load inactive visibility policy or full-provider/live-service/hosted certification.

AWS↔SQLite routed hard-delete replication verified (2026-10-16): expanded existing four sync/async×primary V2 lifecycle cases replace direct-provider cleanup with actual routed hard deletion AFTER soft deletion. With replication enabled, result is true/no error/no warning; official DynamoDB SDK consistent GetItem reads independently prove absent avatar/detail items, and SQLite loads prove both records absent. Native17/17 passes10s, zero skips, retaining all prior race/acknowledgement-fence tests. This closes the tested hard-after-soft replication evidence gap, not fresh-active hard deletion under every race, durable recovery, live AWS/IAM, full interface/history or hosted release gates. No production change required.

Google↔SQLite stronger routed lifecycle proof (2026-10-16): existing four primary-provider×sync/async V2 scenarios now seed a nonempty stored actor, assert returned committed evidence and both providers' canonical profile/detail actor/timestamp, then route hard deletion through V2 with replication enabled. Independent Firestore document existence and SQLite loads prove both records removed in BOTH stores, no warning/error. Native suite remains5/5 passes14s, zero skips (expanded assertions, not extra top-level tests). Existing incoming tombstone idempotence/conflict cases retained. No new production change needed; narrows prior hard-delete replication and nonempty actor evidence gaps, not durable recovery/all-interface/live service/hosted certification.

Google Firestore deletion actor regression fixed (2026-10-16): two new sync/async native tests failed before the fix because the shared transaction constructed an active profile's tombstone with the default empty actor, discarding existing DeletedByAvatarId. One source change passes the stored actor into the existing tombstone constructor. Same native suite now5/5 passes14s, zero skips, retaining prior3 broad tests; new cases independently read canonical profile/detail documents after soft delete/repeat and assert exact actor/timestamp, retained profile/XP321/Karma29/inventory, then paired hard removal. Official retained Firestore emulator, not live Google IAM/service proof. This closes stored-actor preservation only; full interfaces/history/save races/acknowledgement recovery/Storage/BigQuery/hosted release remain open. Existing direct DeleteAvatar API has no actor parameter; this change preserves stored lifecycle context, it does not invent authentication propagation.

Azure Blob lifecycle evidence (2026-10-16): current native TRX proves12/12 passed, zero failures/skips. Shared existing GUID deletion path now coordinates existing avatar/detail blobs using canonical snapshots and per-blob ETag conditions; incoming committed tombstones preserve timestamp/actor and target-specific fields. Actual V2 Azure↔SQLite sync/async soft/hard replication passes. Official Azure SDK requests to reserved unreachable loopback10003 after the first real blob acknowledgement prove partial-state errors carry exact acknowledged paths/original cause and fence both failover and replication (zero SQLite manager requests). Independent raw reads verify partial source state and unchanged replica. This is NOT a cross-blob transaction: missing-detail creation races, durable recovery, physical acknowledgement loss and all-interface/live Azure/hosted release verification remain OPEN. Never report pair atomicity or full certification.

## Current user-directed priority (2026-10-16)

Work order: MongoDB/SQLite → Ethereum/Solana → IPFS → AWS/Azure/Google Cloud/CosmosDB → Polygon/Arbitrum → Neo4j → remaining chains → SOLID/ActivityPub. This order supersedes the older goal wording that foregrounds Cosmos blockchain, Hedera, Sui and TRON. It is the user's product priority, not a claim of measured market-share rankings. Lower-priority chain work must not imply the higher-priority providers are complete: no provider is fully certified; retain all open release gates and distinguish native SDK tests from hosted/full-interface verification.

Shared Web3Core/Polygon native NFT burn updated (2026-10-16): existing Web3NftTokenId parser now validates request.TokenId, removing GUID parsing. Burn uses the existing SDK SendAndWaitForReceiptAsync pipeline via explicit burn(uint256) extension: shared mutation gate, pending nonce, chain-bound signature verification, original signed hash, native preparation callback and single submit/receipt observation. Matching configured signer/target retains its preparation scope; different target/signer uses existing ForNftTransaction, which REFUSES rebinding under active preparation rather than bypassing it. Explicit owner key/actual owner and contract validated; no platform fallback. Web3Erc721Burn verifies mined receipt/hash and exact contract/owner/zero/token Transfer event; absent event retains actual receipt/hash in reconciliation exception. SendNFT responses now expose normalized native TokenId too.

Actual Polygon wrapper new native scenario passes as part of Web3 native3/3 in25s, zero skips:0/uint256max burns preserve source application GUID distinction, original signed-preparation hashes match receipts, callback chain/target verified, independent nonexistent-token reads, changed target during preparation rejected with no callback/nonce change, explicit external contract burn works outside that scope, mined no-op rejected with hash/unchanged owner. Existing Ethereum93/93pass21s and59contract assertions retained. Test compile namespace omission fixed before native run; no SDK/runtime installation. This closes the old shared GUID-parse gap for tested wrapper/paths, NOT all wrapper networks, alternate-owner signing branch, durable burn outbox/admission/settlement, forged-event noncompliant contracts, finality/reorg recovery, burn manager/HTTP or bridge custody-release. Base ERC721 does not require burn extension; unsupported contracts fail rather than use another ABI. Seven clean publishes and hosted gates remain open; no full-provider certification/merge/deployment.

ONODE manager forwards persisted native ID, rejecting an explicitly conflicting ID; full manager lookup/HTTP coverage remains open. First new burn test exposed SDK custom-error versus generic-RPC assertion mismatch; corrected to exact SDK error/encoded token, not relaxed to arbitrary failure. Test-only retained ERC721 fixture adds owner-only burn extension; production Web3Core contract NOT changed or deployed. Burn extension is not part of base ERC721; unsupported target contracts fail, no ABI fallback.

Supersedes earlier Ethereum GUID-hash burn gap only for this tested direct provider path. NFT burn manager replay defect fixed (2026-10-16): actual NFTManager.BurnWeb3NFTAsync loop repeatedly called provider BurnNFTAsync on errors/throws while nominally waiting for burn confirmation, with blocking sleeps, dropped provider exception/evidence and extra sleep even after success. Manager now resolves NFT/provider once and calls production NftBurnExecution.ExecuteOnceAsync once; returns original provider result unchanged (including warnings/native transaction/reconciliation evidence), explicit error for null outcome, preserves thrown cause without resubmission. Unavailable provider and failed NFT lookup now preserve original cause rather than empty apparent success/null-message dereference. Existing WaitTillNFTBurnt fields are forwarded to the provider; future waiting must query an identified receipt, never repeat mutation. ONODE Core4d4b122fd70421ad8bfcfedc5c0b4fd12161556b pushed, parent pin/gitlink advanced together; no deploy/merge/sponsored execution enabled.

Neo4j soft-delete implementation repaired, native verification OPEN (2026-10-15): all8 public avatar deletion entry points (GUID/provider-key/email/username, sync/async) now delegate to one canonical snapshot implementation. ## Priority and shared EVM NFT bridge gate (2026-10-15)

Human-confirmed execution order: MongoDB/SQLite, Ethereum/Solana, IPFS, AWS/Azure/Google Cloud/Cosmos DB, Polygon/Arbitrum, Neo4j, remaining blockchains, then SOLID/ActivityPub. This is the OASIS work priority, not a measured popularity ranking. Cosmos DB is not Cosmos blockchain. Remaining Hedera/TRON/Sui/Cosmos blockchain work stays in scope but must not displace unfinished higher-priority gates. No provider has full end-to-end certification.

New source audit: `Providers/Blockchain/NextGenSoftware.OASIS.API.Providers.Web3CoreOASIS/src/Web3CoreOASISBaseProvider.NFTToken.cs` has an unresolved bridge implementation gap, distinct from storage routing:

- `WithdrawNFTAsync` accepts a string token ID, but maps non-GUID IDs to a deterministic GUID derived only from provider and token-contract address. Different ERC-721 numeric token IDs in the same collection therefore lose their identity in this projection. The supplied sender address/private key are checked for presence but not passed into the constructed lock request.
- `DepositNFTAsync` constructs a mint request containing only the receiver address. It does not establish source transaction finality, asset/token identity, metadata provenance or replay protection; its own comments leave source metadata retrieval for future implementation. A successful downstream mint would not prove a valid bridge deposit.
- These are source findings, not a live exploit, transaction submission, or completed fix. The shared implementation must not be certified as a fully working NFT bridge on the strength of storage, activation or inactive-provider tests. Determine concrete provider overrides before attributing this to every EVM provider; Ethereum/Polygon shared-base consumers are in the audit scope, while legacy Arbitrum and its optional Web3Core wrapper must remain distinguished.
- Required proof: lossless numeric token IDs through lock/unlock, actual sender authorization, verified source-chain receipt and finality, exact destination asset binding, duplicate-source/replay rejection, real local contract receipts/readback, and negative invalid/unfinalized/wrong-owner cases. Keep WEB4 GUIDs separate from native uint256 token IDs. Do not add GUID coercions, dummy unsupported APIs or a second fallback bridge path.

The existing Polygon unit project still has six stale manager-level NFT calls and does not compile. Fixing that test-layer drift alone will not satisfy this bridge gate. No public-chain RPC, gas spending, runtime install, new worktree or ledger reset was performed for this audit.

Build now reports six remaining stale NFT/GeoNFT calls instead of eight. Project STILL DOES NOT COMPILE; no tests passed/excluded/skipped to hide it. Remaining legacy LoadNFT(Guid/hash), avatar-owned NFT/GeoNFT lists and GeoNFT placement are not methods on current IOASISNFTProvider. Its actual read boundary is LoadOnChainNFTDataAsync(token-contract-address); WEB4 GUID/hash/placement semantics must not be silently coerced into on-chain addresses or dummy provider methods. Migrate intended manager-level coverage to the real manager API and add actual provider-interface cases separately; old universal "not supported" expectations also need review against current implemented capabilities. Existing direct Polygon native scenario remains separate evidence, not a green claim for this legacy project.

IPFS selected graph saves implemented (2026-10-15 local/16UTC): prior public single/bulk saves only wrote roots; canonical OasisJson excludes child navigation, so accepted recursion/depth flags did not persist child records. New selected-graph planner validates full selected input and stages canonical snapshots before native writes, retaining prior nonempty-ID contract. Rejects negative depths, null/repeated/cyclic nodes, duplicate IDs and conflicting parents as terminal payload errors before CID writes. Single/bulk sync/async share execution; selected children get parent IDs, depth/recursive/saveChildren/initial-depth control selection, source storage keys published after confirmed node success. Known failed parents skip descendants; continueOnError controls subsequent independent nodes, but reconciliation-required exceptions stop immediately. Earlier acknowledged CIDs wrap failures for replay fencing. Reuses canonical encrypted record/index publication, not a parallel serializer/storage path.

Shared single-record writer prepares the complete BSON through the actual collection serializer BEFORE dispatch for inserts and replacements, then writes those exact bytes/doc through the real Mongo SDK once. Payload-serialization phase is tracked separately from reads; only that known pre-dispatch encoding failure is classified terminal HYPERDRIVE_PAYLOAD_INVALID, not uncertain native completion or a provider outage.

## Current priority and Mongo collection-query evidence (2026-10-15)

User-confirmed work order: MongoDB/SQLite → Ethereum/Solana → IPFS → AWS/Azure/GoogleCloud/CosmosDB → Polygon/Arbitrum → Neo4j → remaining chains → SOLID/ActivityPub. This is the requested implementation priority, not a measured popularity ranking. Returning to a smaller chain never proves higher-priority providers complete. The active product goal text still names smaller chains; the available goal tool cannot edit objective text. This document records the governing work order without narrowing the full completion requirements.

Mongo public sync/async collection, parent-GUID/native-parent-key and scalar/dictionary metadata queries now share the existing graph projector. Root type/version/metadata filters select roots only; provider-linked descendants of different types/versions remain visible, soft-deleted descendants are excluded, and root/descendant queries share one native snapshot session. Flat/embedded reads do not require a snapshot session. Existing metadata filters now use explicit Mongo `$literal` expressions for keys and values, preserving dotted and leading-dollar dictionary keys/values rather than interpreting them as expression paths.

Final fixed `mongo-sqlite-v2-current.trx` at 2026-10-15 18:01:38 reports total4/passed4, zero failures/skips, adapter finish22.49s. Children deliberately differ from root type/version and embedded names differ from native names. Earlier test-only compile errors (tuple naming and request parent field) were corrected to the actual public contract before the final successful run.

Requested version filters the stored root, not historical versions. Session-disposal failure has explicit causal handling but was not fault-injected. No full certification or merge claimed. No installs/downloads or duplicate environments.

Provider priority: MongoDB/SQLite, Ethereum/Solana, IPFS, AWS/Azure/GoogleCloud/CosmosDB, Polygon/Arbitrum, Neo4j, remaining chains, then SOLID/ActivityPub. Original CosmosBlockChain/Hashgraph/Sui/TRON requirements remain in scope; they do not supersede these priorities.

Mongo holon transaction-owner audit for import (2026-10-14, parent588024caf): inspected actual SaveHolons/Async and HolonRepository partials. Priority predictive-routing ownership audit (2026-10-13, parent88e31642b): inspected actual Core router/engine and WEB4 controller, GraphQL and gRPC call sites. WEB4 callers use PredictiveFailoverEngine.Instance; each OASISHyperDrive constructor instead creates a new engine with its ProviderManager. Override recommendations are stored in an instance-owned concurrent dictionary, and the router never invokes PredictAndPreventFailuresAsync on its private engine. Thus endpoint-generated recommendations do not reach request routing; a successful prediction endpoint is not proof of preventive routing. A second gap remains: RouteToProvider/Async can resolve a predictive alternative internally without returning its identity to RouteRequest/Async, leaving failover-origin and replication-primary bookkeeping on the original selection. Ordinary successful failover tracking was fixed and tested earlier; that evidence does not cover this predictive path. Required next work: one engine owner per ProviderManager (isolated managers must not share global recommendations), request-local actual-provider propagation, and real Mongo/SQLite tests proving endpoint/engine-to-router propagation, correct replication exclusion/repair, failure-origin handling and manager isolation. No predictive implementation or end-to-end certification claimed by this audit; no runtime/download/environment allocation or merge.

Human priority order remains MongoDB/SQLite, Ethereum/Solana, IPFS, AWS/Azure/GoogleCloud/CosmosDB, Polygon/Arbitrum, Neo4j, remaining chains, SOLID/ActivityPub. The persistent goal's older four-chain wording does not override this newer instruction. Higher-priority providers are not all fully verified; finish and prove their required interfaces/routing before resuming lower-priority chain gates. Hedera funding work remains deferred at the user's request.

SQLite failed-delete checkpoint: all four GUID/provider-key sync/async delete paths preserve the original exception and detach only the owned failed entity. Real SQLite trigger-rejection tests prove a later save does not silently replay a rejected hard delete, then successful hard deletion removes the record. Soft-delete rejection, concurrency and other full-provider gates remain open.

Provider-key deletes now report IsDeleted/DeletedCount rather than IsSaved. Real SQLite integration suite12/12 passed, including sync/async GUID delete and reopen persistence. ## Work order

1. Return to the user-approved provider order: MongoDB/SQLite, Ethereum/Solana, IPFS, clouds, L2s, Neo4j, remaining chains, protocol integrations. Current SQLite fixes are incomplete; historical passing subsets do not certify priority providers. Preserve native uncertainty fences and the original four-chain completion scope.
2. Close unified encryption configuration/policy and live registration gaps without exposing legacy exempt private fields on-chain or breaking database indexes.
3. Complete live interfaces/options and failure/distribution matrices; preserve uncertain transaction ownership. Resume funded Hedera only when adequately funded, without real-money fees.
4. Prove authenticated API and published Linux packaging in approved existing infrastructure. Do not create another Docker/runtime environment by default.
5. Complete production native recovery in the actual execution host with original signed evidence and approved valuation; no guessed resolver, zero-charge inference or rebroadcast. Do not let lower-priority chain work indefinitely displace the agreed order.
6. Audit every original requirement against current authoritative evidence. Merge/push to dev and promote only after all required gates pass; current green subsets are insufficient.

# IPFS parent-key lookup checkpoint — 2026-10-15

Fixed parent-provider-key lookup to resolve the canonical holon parent identity, then select its children rather than select the parent itself. `HolonType.All` now works through the GUID path; synchronous parent wrappers forward version/options. Unsupported historical parent-key requests fail explicitly instead of silently returning current data. Graph option implementation remains an open gate.

Extended the real offline Kubo scenario: encrypted child storage; async and sync parent-key lookup return the child, not the parent; a leaf returns no children; historical requests are rejected. Result: 1/1 selected native scenario passed, zero skips, 582 ms. This is not proof of HyperDrive routing/failover/replication/load-balancing or full interface completeness.

Initial test exposed a runner build-order defect: it compiled only the consumer while retaining a stale provider assembly. Rebuilt the producer; the runner now builds producer before consumer with existing cached dependencies. Retained that failure report and encrypted fixture as `TestResults/IPFSCurrent/stale-producer.trx` and `stale-producer-dna.json`, without overwriting uncertain evidence. Successful rerun unpinned its own CIDs, GC removed 24 unpinned blocks, and the owned daemon stopped. No download, new environment, ledger reset or public fees. Free bytes 89960452096 → 89959718912.
